## Pharaxes Zora

Pharaxes Zora was a cleric of Bane who was stationed in Elmwood during the Rage of Dragons of, in an attempt to conquer it for the Zhentarim. He was slain by Pavel Shemov's party.

Description
Barrel-chested, Pharaxes' uncombed, greasy hair and stained clothing made him resemble a violent ruffian more than a priest.

Personality
Pharaxes got a cynical thrill out of lying to people he perceived as unable to respond. He was quick to resort to insults, and swift to punish perceived disobedience.

Abilities
Pharaxes entered into a spell duel with Pavel Shemov.

Possessions
In addition to his armor, his holy symbol of Bane and a morningstar, Pharaxes carried a ring of magic detection, which he used to determine that Karasendrieth was probably concealing some possessions from them.

Activities
Officially a "customs agent" in Elmwood, Pharaxes was part of a very official-sounding scheme to rob every traveler on behalf of an alliance around the Moonsea to fight the Rage of Dragons. The alliance, yet to materialize, had Zhentil Keep at its head and had granted itself the right to arbitrarily tax travelers passing through Elmwood.

He was also the captain of a warship, the Dagger, out of Yulash.

History
Pharaxes was slain in Tarsakh 11, while in a spell duel against Pavel Shemov that started after his party attacked him in protection of Karasendrieth's possessions, which they expected would be confiscated by the customs agent.

Phelpar polymorphed his rival Selvar into a frog for several years. He used this opportunity to steal all of Selvar's spells and magic items and hid them in a secret cache in a tomb in the Rathgaunt Hills in the Shaar. Selvar was somehow able to transform himself back to his natural form and tracked Phelpar down to exact his revenge. ## Philock

Philock was found in a large rift located in the center of a massive system of caverns, under the eastern part of the Wood of Sharp Teeth in the Western Heartlands.
Description
The buildings of Philock were built through the floor of the canyon and stretched down to the lower reaches of the rift. As of, the former city was in ruins, buried under tons of rubble from the frequent cave-ins and landslides over the centuries.

Some adventuring parties have attempted to remove reach the buildings of Philock, in an attempt to find ancient Netherese artifacts. Every attempt to excavate the ruins have failed, although a large drow expedition, and their small army of slave labor, had more success than most. However, much of their slaves contracted lungrot after breaching into a stairwell.

Netherese refugees established Philock following the Fall of Netheril in the .

Relationships
She served at the Tilverton temple alongside her fellow priest Rull.

History

During the Time of Troubles in the, Phylanna spent a passionate night with the mercenary Kelemvor Lyonsbane. The morning after, she was dazed by the jackalwere Torrence, who attempted to lure out Kelemvor's lycanthropic panther form. Phylanna was aghast at Kelemvor's eventual transformation, telling her once-lover he was accursed and should leave Tilverton for good.

## Phull Villa

Phull Villa was the family estate of the Waterdhavian noble house Phull .

Location

The Phull Villa was located in the North Ward of Waterdeep, on the corner of Phull Lane and Trollkill Street. It stood across from the Snome Villa and to the back of it was Anteos Villa.

Structure
Like many Waterdhavian villas in the North Ward, this was a walled estate featuring a landscaped gardenEvidence of this information can be seen in how the villa is depicted on maps. with elaborate adornments. Inside the walls of the estate were two high-quality buildings that stood three stories and one story tall, these being the mansion proper and a guesthouse respectively. Their estate also featured a cellar with a vault.

Inhabitants
Besides the members of House Phull, this estate was inhabited by five watchspiders who guarded the family's vault. Whenever there was an intruder the watchspiders would attack in two pairs, while the fifth would ring a bell on the wall near the entrance.

Phrenic creatures were otherwise ordinary creatures that had psionic abilities. Any animal found to be a phrenic creature was considered to be a magical beast.

Phrenic creatures manifested psionic abilities ranging in power from defensive precognition and force screen to ultrablast. Additionally, they had some inherent resistant to psionic power.

## Phaudrantanthildrar

Phaudrantanthildrar, or Phaudron for short, was a steel dragon who lived on the continent of Arandron on Toril.

Relations
Phaudron's mate was Malavauvrantarandra (Malavauvra), who was aged and unwell. Malavauvra had laid a number of clutches of eggs in her lifetime, but her last with Phaudron was in poor shape: most of the young were sickly and died as hatchlings or within their first year. Only Velsaertirden, Rauvrahrileenatandrar, and Torthalindrarron survived.

Lair
The family laired within the castle of Steelspires, which had been built for them by stone giants. It stood on a high, bare rocky ridge behind the bustling port city of Haunvaerune.

Personality
Phaudron was a withdrawn, scholarly type who was focused on his research.

Activities
He had a keen interest in magic that would extend a dragon's life and vitality without limit, and without becoming a dracolich or employing other forms of necromancy. To this end, he constantly developed and experimented with arcane magic and regularly traveled far and wide to discover new magics and obtain rare substances for material components.

History
Some time around the 8 century DR, Phaudron went off on another expedition, but could not find the rare component he was after. While he was away, his daughter Rauvrahreene discovered his notes, made copies, and slipped away to her lair to devise a way to reverse her father's spells. When Phaudron returned weary to Steelspires, she was waiting—Rauvrahreene secretly cast the reversed spells and slew her father. She then murdered her mother in her sleep the same way and seized control of Steelspires, turning it against her brothers.

## Phastal Street

Phastal Street was a minor, corduroy-log paved road located in the Sea Ward of Waterdeep, not far from the Trollwall.

Geography
The street ran roughly east-west, beginning at Stormstar's Ride at the west end and terminating at Heroes' Walk—the road that circumnavigated Heroes' Garden—in the east. Along the way, it intersected with the south end of Sanborim Street just before reaching Heroes' Walk.

History

In Eleint of the, investigations after the fact found that an apprentice wizard attempted a fire spell that got out of control. The apprentice died in the conflagration and several homes, businesses, and villas on Phastal Street were consumed in the blaze as the Watchful Order of Magists and Protectors attempted to put out the fire and limit the damage.

Every full moon since the disaster, a spectral image of a woman looking out a window appeared in the air above Phastal Street, a ghostly remnant of a second-story window in a villa that was destroyed in the fire. The woman and the window appeared to be illuminated by the soft glow of a lantern sitting on the sill. The apparition could only be seen when facing it directly from Phastal Street. It was not visible from the side or the back when viewed from Stormstar's Ride.

References

## Phutan

Phutan was a small kingdom in Kara-Tur, neighboring Tabot. Its capital city was Bhutan.The place is called only "Phutan" in Kara-Tur: The Eastern Realms Volume I, but only "Bhutan" appears on all maps of the region. While this could be an error or variation in spelling, there is no actual contradiction: the maps specifically label the city as "Bhutan", not the country.

Geography
Phutan occupied a wedge of land between the Po Yul Dzayul Range to the north and the Wu Pi Te Shao Mountains to the southwest. To the southeast was the border with Tabot, leaving it quite locked in. Moreover, the land was covered entirely in dense bamboo forest, bisected by a river that flowed into Tabot and was ultimately a tributary of the Hungtse River in Shou Lung. In the middle of this, beside the river, stood the city of Bhutan. The only other river of note was the misty Wohani River, which meandered through the foothills of the Po Yul Dzayul in the north before entering Tabot.

Relations
The monks of Tabot, no friends to Phutan, saw it as lawless land that gave sanctuary to outlaws from Tabot, T'u Lung, and Shou Lung, and that its "heathen king" decreed lamas and holy men be killed on sight. There were constant conflicts between the Phutanese and the Tabotans at Hoshki Gompa and Buchan Do Gompa over the border. Passage through Tabot to Phutan was restricted, but the rebellious Lady Holang of Pim Tung in Tabot would allow free passage upriver to Phutan for those who paid.

History
Prehistory

Phutan shared its early past with Tabot. Originally, the land was the domain of barbarian kings and tribal chiefs with their own noble class. They built cities in the lowlands and followed a heathen faith. In their founding myth, millennia previously, the moon descended to the earth one night in the form of a beautiful woman. As she wandered through the night, all the white tigers of the land gathered at her side and were entranced by her. She touched her hand to the foreheads of nineteen of them and they were transformed into human men of strength and intelligence. After the moon women returned to the sky, the new men performed heroic deeds and founded the various clans. One of these clans founded Phutan, the others the various kingdoms of Tabot.

But unlike Tabot, Phutan seems never to have been occupied by the exiled Shou monks and their monasteries between  and, nor to have fallen under the sway of the High Lord of Oceans in .The history of Phutan and its split from Tabot is unknown, but its lack of religious sites on the map, different faith, and conflicts suggest this account.

Modern History
In, Buchan Do lamas reported a Phutanese army assembling at the border. Tabot went on a war footing, mustering soldiers in the area and ordering conscription in a few towns, hiring mercenaries, and accepting volunteers from among the temple guards.

## Phags-pa

Phags-pa was a hare hengeyokai monk living in the forested Valley of Lo Chi in the mid–14 century DR.

History
Phags-pa was born to Notia T'ong, who acted as the guardian of his tribe against the cruel shukenja Ko Hung Cho and his monks from Lo Chi monastery, who over the years attempted to hunt the hare hengeyokai down and force them into working their mines. At some point, Notia T'ong he sent Phags-pa to go learn the ways of the Lo Chi monastery, as he knew almost nothing about them himself. Taking the form of a human, he did so, but was eventually cast out by them.

Later, around, a group of adventurers from Shou Lung stumbled upon Phags-pa in a snowdrift while wandering the Wu Pi Te Shao Mountains in search of the fabled Flowers of Flame. When they found him Phags-pa's hands and feet were bloody stumps, his body having become frostbitten and lost a great deal of blood. Still clinging to life, Phags-pa told the adventurers his name, the tragic tale of his people, and warned them to beware of Ko Hung Cho and the fire spirit. His last words to them were to go find his people through a gate to the west. Following this, Phags-pa transformed into a hare and breathed his last breath.

## Phambra Chalyphar

Phambra Chalyphar was the Magister for six months in .

Appearance
Phambra had glossy black hair. She was quite beautiful.

Personality
She was governed by her ambition, with a malicious tongue and a hatred of all those who "knew too much about her".

Activities
Phambra was known to attend Magefairs after her death, where she frightened young mages that made moves on her; this gave her the nickname she was known by.

History
She was a local sorceress where one of Hortil Droone's apprentices lived; she noticed him visiting her. After scrying on him, she took him to be a mage of much power and magical wealth, setting up an ambush to "slay and seize".

She remained the Magister only long enough to slay two wizards, leave the belongings of one of them to his apprentice so as to placate Mystra, and then lose her next spell duel, which was against the significantly more expert Arghorst Talandor.

After her death, she became a shadowstaff, whose appearance was mostly the same as in life.

## Phelarnden Ghemelt

Phelandern Ghemelt was the Magister for four months in .

Appearance
He was described as "darkly handsome".

Personality
Phelandern sneered much, and he was fond of sarcasm.

History
After being offered the office of the Magister, Phelandern decided to try and manipulate other factions of mages, such as the Red Wizards of Thay, the Arcane Brotherhood, or the Twisted Rune, into stalemates, so they could not focus their force on him. He also had plans to draw them into an attack on Halruaa, which he hoped to observe or even use as cover to steal some of their magic. He also coveted a number of fabled orbs of power, supposedly owned by Alair Nalabukhar, of the Calishite Nalabukharn family.

Before he could get much of his schemes into motion, however, he was overwhelmed by challengers from Calimshan and Thay, destroying first his tower in wave attacks, and then a number of manors he seized. He was finally torn apart over the Shining Sea, near an island home of his', by Veldribold of Calimport, who died too soon after to inherit the mantle of the Magister.

Rumors
Phelandern actually knew only rumors of the orbs of power he coveted.

## Phaerdinzer

The Phaerdinzer clan was a family of magically powerful individuals extant in the late 14 century DR.

Appearance
The Phaerdinzers were sharp-featured, with unusual eye colors such as emerald, ice-blue, and gray, and hair most often auburn, red, or brown.

History
The clan had been fostered by Maxiladanarr Torstren during his reign as the Magister, from  to . He determined that some archmage had experimented on them "until their veins ran with magic". As this was not a normal occurrence, he considered there was something special about their experimentation or about their makeup.

More immediately before his fostering, they had been shopkeepers, mostly in the furniture and medicine business, for a long time. Their members had practiced magic, but took care to hide the true extent of their powers.

Structure
As of, the clan was led by Othlo Phaerdinzer and Jasurra Phaerdinzer, who remained in their Tallstones mansion in Athkatla, Amn. Their most likely successor was Dartabba Shoukyn.

The clan counted about six hundred people, of which two hundred may have been mages of some accomplishment, as most never developed their power beyond a handful of spells.

Powers
The Phaerdinzers were never in any risk of their body failing while midway through a transformation. They also had the ability to use a number of minor magics at will, such as a form of silence that affected only them, a short-range form of telekinesis out to, and the ability to levitate themselves or feather fall. About half of them also had one more power, like dancing lights, invisibility, detect thoughts, detect invisibility, locate object, spider climb, or sleep.

Reputation
Most people saw the Phaerdinzers as ambitious merchants who just loved their work.

Phaerdinzers never admitted to any power, making excuses for it by blaming it on sorcerous wares, for instance. They thought their powers were probably a prank from the gods, maybe one that they'd be let in on some day a few generations hence.

## Phaen's robe of rags

Phaen's robe of rags was an ordinary-looking yet enchanted robe that once belonged to Phaen of the Rags in the early 14 century DR.

Description
Phaen's robe of rags was just that, a robe sewn together of numerous mismatched ragged strips of cloth. Despite being a magic item, it looked like something a beggar from Luskan could wear. On the inside, the robe was lined with bear's pelt.

Powers
Enchantments were weaved into the robe's fabric. It granted the wearer a +2 defensive enchantment and protected them from the normal and magical cold. Phaen's robe of rags was masterfully enchanted, and its magics were virtually undetectable even to most experienced wizards.

History
In the, this robe was worn by Phaen of the Rags, an outcast mage from Luskan's Arcane Brotherhood. He eventually joined the Legion of the Chimera that threatened the Ten Towns of Icewind Dale with destruction. In Mirtul of the, Phaen pretended to be a lowly beggar in Targos while spying for the Legion and magically transporting goblin marauders past the city's walls and fortifications. When his allegiance was revealed, Phaen was slain by a band of mercenaries from Targos and the enchanted robes left his possession.

## Phantom fungus

Phantom fungi were a breed of predatory, mobile fungus feared by those underground for their natural invisibility.

Description
Ordinarily, the only details indicating the presence of phantom fungi were the sounds of soft squelching footfalls accompanied by a strange smell of mold. If rendered visible, phantom fungi appeared as a brown or greenish brown fungal mass supported by four stumpy legs. A cluster of nodules rested atop its main body and its gaping maw was lined with rows of teeth.

Abilities

Phantom fungi were constantly and permanently under the effects of greater invisibility, allowing them to attack while invisible. Invisibility purge spells could not dispel this status, but they did become visible a minute after being slain. Phantom fungi were also adept at moving quietly.

Combat
Phantom fungi might wait for a meal to come to them but usually quietly hunted for prey, roaming the world until they came upon suitable targets. While not especially intelligent, they had certain instinctive strategies and were capable of determining easy from difficult prey. They would attack lone individuals almost anywhere, but preferred open spaces when tackling groups, moving around slightly between each bite from its many teeth to make itself harder to find.

Ecology
Phantom fungi were solitary subterranean creatures. There were Lowerdark variants of phantom fungi serving the druidic Circle of the Depths in the Underdark city of Fluvenilstra.

The plane of shadow was known to host shadow phantom fungi. Fiendish and half-fiend phantom fungi were also abundant in Shedaklah, the realm of the Demon Queen of Fungi Zuggtmoy. Phantom fungi, being fungal monsters, were among the favorite and most commonly summoned minions of Zuggtmoy, and among her faithful fungoid servants who reveled in the unnaturally warm dampness and actively sought to destroy oozes that emerged from Juiblex's portion of the realm.

Biology
Phantom fungi had low-light vision, their sensory organs being the nodule cluster atop their main body.

## Phase potion

Phase potions were a type of magical potion similar in nature to oils of etherealness.

Powers
As their name suggested, phase potions temporarily granted their imbibers an imitation of a phase spider's ability to shift their bodies in and out of phase between the Prime Material and the Ethereal plane, making them otherwise impervious to harm on the former by all but those who could reach into the Ethereal plane themselves. The imbiber was also able to phase out with them up to  of material matter. Objects could be taken without any issue, though living creatures could resist against the effect.

The effective length of a phase potion's effects was anywhere from around 8 to 40 minutes. And whilst an imbiber's body was in phase with the Prime Material, a trace of the phase potion's portal to the Ethereal plane was visible in the form of a fiery nimbus of white fire surrounding them.

Notably, the spell phase door interfered with the effects of phase potions, forcing their imbiber to remain in phase with the Prime Material for a period of seven minutes.

Creation
Creating a phase potion required either the blood of a phase spider or the concentrated juices of a rare species of tiny, button-shaped silvery white fungi that grew underground.

History
Sometime around the mid-14 century DR, phase potions were used in the North by the Cult of the White Bat, who considered them essential to their plans. Their usage of phase potions when conducting midnight enslavement raids upon isolated settlements led to their activity being reported as sightings of dwarven ghosts, appearing out of nowhere and walking through walls.

## Pheljara's wand

Pheljara's wand was a wand used by the Harpers.

Powers
When the designated command word was spoken, Pheljara's wand sprung into action, propelling a dull black cord with a sticky end from its tip. The wand's enchantment ensured that the cord traveled in a perfectly straight line, unaffected by gravity, winds, or any movements of the wand itself. It continued on this course, extending outward for a maximum distance of  or until it collided with a solid object. If no obstruction was encountered, the cord remained rigid, connected solely to the wand. It could be placed across a gap or between facing windows of adjacent structures and could even be used to climb vertically by securely wedging the wand to prevent the line from falling.

Upon striking a solid object, the cord adhered to it with remarkable strength, akin to the adhesive properties of a wand of viscid globs. The bond could only be dissolved by alcohol, and attempting to forcibly break free may result in joint dislocation for trapped individuals. The cord possessed a non-reflective surface, maintaining its texture even when wet, providing a rough texture akin to suede or bark for enhanced traction and grip. It possessed exceptional durability, capable of supporting weights of up to  or the equivalent of ten fully armored human warriors with their typical equipment, without breaking. The cord was impervious to acid and other corrosive substances, and it exhibited resistance to fire and prevented the spread of flames along its length.

The cord eventually dissipated into nothingness, along with its sticky end, once the magic expired or when a being touched the wand and uttered a second command word.

## Philosophy of Kara-Tur

The Philosophy of Kara-Tur was a tome of knowledge about the eastern continent of Kara-Tur, penned sometime before 1281 DR.

Content
One notable excerpt from the tome was a story from the eastern lands about a conversation between two chaste shukenja. When the two chaste men came across a beautiful woman taking shelter under a tree by the side of a road, she noticed the man and called out to them. The beautiful woman asked the men to carry her across a stream. The first shukenja immediately set out to aid her while the other man silently protested. The first man carried the woman in his arms with ease across the stream and returned to the road to go his way. The second man stayed silent until the beautiful woman was out of sight and then spoke out: "We shukenja are not supposed to have contact with women! Why did you pick that woman up so casually and carry her across the stream?" His monastic brother replied: "Brother, I set the woman down long ago. Why is it that you have not?"

History
By the, this book on philosophy found its way to several locations in the northwest Faerûn, namely the monastic retreat within the Dragon's Eye on the Spine of the World, and in the, in the library of the Black Raven Monastery, in the mountains above the Ice Lakes and the Black Raven River.

## Pheldred

Pheldred was a Red Wizard of Thay in the mid-14 century.

Description
For a long time, his arms kept changing into manacles. He had dark hair and glittering black eyes.

Abilities
During his duel with Aumlar Chaunthoun, Pheldred cast a number of spells, including one which launched a shimmering sword, one which created an advancing strip of drifting silver sparks, apparently called deathwall, and another of his spells generated a spiraling silver energy that closed around its target, with unknown effects; the spiral was vulnerable to some wands. He used some manner of shieldings, which blocked numerous spells from wands, but worked ablatively, being slowly worn down by volume. Some of his shieldings could be made to expand and turn away magic. Another of his spells drank the power from spells around him and even wands, powering new spellblades with them.

Relationships
He sought revenge against Aumlar Chaunthoun, a Zhentarim mage. The mages Thavaun and Hulrivior of Triel counted him as their own. Korthauvar Hammantle and Hlael Toraunt were aware of him and his history.

Possessions
He wore two rings, dark maroon robes, and a richly made belt, in which he held two daggers and a whip.

History
Pheldred had been cursed at some point by Aumlar Chaunthoun, so that his arms kept changing into manacles. He spent some years with this handicap, until he was finally able to find the names by which he could command the fiend into which Aumlar Chathoun had bound the curse. The Zhentarim knew he'd managed to slip out of too many deadly situations unscathed.

In late, Pheldred ambushed Aumlar Chaunthoun, who was undercover in a caravan under Orthil Voldovan, en route from Scornubel to Triel, on the way to Waterdeep. He was eventually overmatched and forced to flee.

## Pharaulee

Pharaulee was an innkeeper in Scornubel.

Abilities
She apparently had the ability to cast her sight from other people's faces, in the process displaying the face of an old woman on top of theirs.

Description
The old woman's face that peered out of the faces of other people when she looked through them may have been her own. If so, she had old, sad eyes, and a lushly beautiful face, large and gray-haired.

Relationships
Pharaulee was usually contacted through Rildra, and protected by Ulburt. After Rildra's death, she was contacted directly, at least to Marlel.

She was the innkeeper at the Stormy Tankard in Scornubel, charging three silver pieces per night. She was almost certainly a Harper.

Pharaulee was a contact for Marlel when he brought Shandril Shessair and Narm Tamaraith to the Stormy Tankard. He meant to talk through Rildra, but had to deal with Pharaulee directly instead, as Rildra had recently perished. She was also the innkeeper at the Tankard.

## Phaedrus

Phaedrus was a hollyphant messenger who served both Lathander and Seker.

Description
As was typical of hollyphants, Phaedrus appeared as a winged, golden-furred elephant measuring  in length. His feathery wings were white, his tusks had a silver-like gleam, and his diamond-esque eyes refracted light in a full rainbow of colors. Owing to a previous encounter with a death knight, his left ear featured a large nick.

Personality
Although cheerful by human standards, Phaedrus was serious and aggressive compared to other hollyphants. He was insightful and his humor had a sardonic streak.

Phaedrus had a vendetta against the undead ever since his confrontation with a death knight. This vendetta was the driving factor behind his service to Lathander and Seker.

Abilities
As a hollyphant, Phaedrus had both magical and psionic abilties. He constantly detected evil within a radius of, and had powers equivalent to the spells banishment, bless, cure serious wounds, flame strike, heal, light, protection from evil, raise dead, and teleport without error. He also had access to the sciences aura sight, mindlink, and precognition, as well as the devotions danger sense, identity penetration, invisibility, know direction, know location, and post-hypnotic suggestion.

Phaedrus could also make the hollyphant's hallmark trumpet-calls, which would manifest as either a concussive blast powerful enough to damage buildings, an eardrum-shattering sound that deafens those caught in its wake, or a shower of "sun-sparkles" composed of positive energy.

Activities
In his capacity as a divine messenger, Phaedrus would typically send adventurers on quests that contributed to the larger struggle against the undead. Typical missions included destroying artifacts of undeath, defeating powerful undead beings, and infiltrating and breaking up undead-oriented organizations such as necromancer cabals and cults to gods of undeath. In order to facilitate these quests, Phaedrus would sometimes lead his agents to magical items useful against the undead, such as amulets versus undead, wands of illumination, sun blades and maces of disruption.

Phaedrus also granted empowering boons to the faithful clerics and paladins of Lathander and Seker, and he warned against the use of animate dead lest they incur the displeasure of these deities.

In the, the city of Baldur's Gate was threatened by the encroaching army of the Cult of the Absolute. The crisis flooded the city with refugees, elevating tensions among its citizens and allowing a new power to take rulership of the city – Lord Gortash. Phyria  eagerly consumed the new lord's propaganda from the curbside preachers and from the pages of the Baldur's Mouth Gazette. ## Phoenix Tail Inn

The Phoenix Tail Inn was the only inn within the Crossroad Keep's walls on the Sword Coast North in the late 14 century DR.

Location
The Phoenix Tail Inn was a three-story building that stood in the southern part of the Crossroad Keep. Its first floor was made out of stone bricks, while the second and third floors were wooden. A single sign hung above the inn's entrance.

Interior
The inn had a spacious dining hall decorated with rustic furniture, carpets, dining tables, and couches and chairs. A stone fireplace had the head of a large wolf mounted over it.

History
The Phoenix Tail Inn was taken over by Sal of Neverwinter in the, after the Crossroad Keep was liberated by the adventurer known as the Kalach-Cha, who was assigned to be its Knight-Captain. Sal was a barkeep who worked at a tavern run by the Knight-Captain's uncle in Neverwinter. Sometime after the Shadow War of 1374 DR, Sal sold the struggling business to Huldur. Under the new ownership, the Phoenix Tail Inn fed local laborers who worked for the Keep and passing adventurers and hunters who dealt with local worg infestation. Some of these individuals were Ulweth, a dwarf worg hunter; Daija of Waukeen who stayed there until she opened a shrine in the fortress; and Nall, an adventurer-turned-militia instructor.

## Pheebs Splayfoot

Pheebs Splayfoot was a hin businessman from the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in late 15 century DR.

Description
Pheebs was a pudgy halfling man with pale skin, blue eyes, and curly black hair.

History
In the, as the city of Baldur's Gate shuddered under the Absolute crisis, certain goods started to become rare and expensive. One such material that skyrocketed in price was saltpeter. Pheebs Splayfoot sought to purchase a shipment of barreled saltpeter from the dwarvish trader Djingo and participated in a bidding war against Kaet Ling and Smiley Gheef. Djingo charged at least 500 gold pieces per barrel, a high price as he knew that once Baldur's Gate was besieged, he'd easily sell it for double the amount to the Gate's steel watcher foundry. Gheef offered 700 gp per barrel, but the bidding war ended abruptly when Kaet Ling offered to pay 1,000 gp per barrel, in cash, no notes. Despite the loss, Gheef was hopeful for future transactions.

Phase disliked Nine-Fingers looking down on the Zhentarim hirelings and was ready to join Roah Moonglow and Friol in their attempts to usurp the Guild for the Zhentarim. ## Pheldemar

Pheldemar Daunthrae was a War Wizard of Cormyr in the late 14 century DR.

Personality
Curious and decisive, Pheldemar used an unknown magical item to destroy a helmed horror just to see what it did.

Description
Pheldemar Daunthrae had steel-gray hair and a face wrinkled enough to resemble a worn boot. While watching over the sanctum, he wore battle-leathers, a leather overcoat, and a long white glove with a flaring cut.

Activities
Pheldemar was part of the retinue protecting Vangerdahast in his sanctum near Mouth o' Gargoyles after retirement.

Relationships
He had some latent conflicts with Brorm, a fellow War Wizard who hurled spells too close to their sanctum. He had some history with Thaerma, which made the prospect of returning to her unpleasant.

Possessions
He used a rod of some sort that allowed him to call shielding magic upon himself. The rod was long and black with tiny spikes and spires, all of them flashing with radiances of different colors. This rod was also able to blast targets with conjured water.

History
Pheldemar Daunthrae was one of the guardians at the sanctum where Vangerdahast was protected in the, after his retirement. He came out to investigate the explosions caused by Aumun Tholant Bezrar and Malakar Surth when they attacked the sanctum as Harnrim Starangh's vanguard. He almost apprehended the men, but they escaped him, leaving Pheldemar badly bruised; he was only able to seize one of their explosives, which he tested against one of the helmed horrors kept around as scouts.

He later gave a debriefing of the situation to Huldyl Rauthur, explaining his testing of the explosives, and the total losses, which amounted to eight helmed horrors. Huldyl ordered him to return to the Royal Court to get his wounds looked at.

History
In the, the Stone Lord began threatening the Guild, and Nine-Fingers needed her old friend's and confidant's ear to pour her thoughts and feelings into. Tut Thistlebog was the Guildmaster's childhood friend and a heavy wine drinker. Keene was worried that the drunk hin would drunkenly run off and challenge the Stone Lord on his own. To protect her fried, Nine-Fingers ordered Phostlethwaite to keep the halfling soaked in wine and busy until the crisis was over. Phostlethwaite pretended to drink alongside Tut while keeping himself sober. When it was revealed that the Stone Lord was none other than Minsc, a personal friend of Baldur's Gate's High Harper, Tut Thistlebog may have revealed Nine-Fingers Keene's plans, with some persuasion from Phostlethwaite to the group of adventurers known as the Absolute Adversaries. Tut blabbed about Keene's plans to ambush her enemy in the Counting House's vault to the group of adventurers known as the Absolute Adversaries.

## Phellep

Phellep was a self-proclaimed prophet who followed the philosophical teachings of providential rejection, who lived long before the late 13 century DR.

History
Phellep preached the philosophical belief that no person's actions had any consequences on humanity, rejecting concepts of good and evil. To apply his beliefs, Phellep created a powerful enchanted weapon he called Inconsequence. The hammer was used to enforce indifference and "encourage" political, social, and moral abstinence. Phellep used Inconsequence to break down the doors of "the dens of fools and mindless men," more commonly known as churches and temples. Phellep likely died some years before the, as Inconsequence left his side.

Gnome Phrennog was among the Baldurians who sought passage to the Upper City, suffering through the long lines and rude Fists. The place was dark and cool.

## Physical Power mantle

The Physical Power mantle was a psionic mantle that granted ardents and divine minds an array of psionic powers and abilities derived from the universal concept of raw physical might, centered around enhancing the manifester's own physical capabilities.

Deities

Abilities
Those who followed the Physical Power mantle gained the unique psionic ability to expend a psionic focus to temporarily enhance their physical capabilities, such as their strength or hardiness.

The psychic aura exuded by divine minds of the Physical Power mantle granted the manifester and any allies within the radius of their aura the phyiscal power to overcome aggressive acts of might, such as attempts to restrain them, trip them, or disarm them of their weapons.

Powers
Novice manifesters obtained adrenaline boost and vigor. Manifesters of some experience gained animal affinity and the power to meld weapons to their body with graft weapon. As a manifester progressed further they learned how to readjust their body with psychofeedback and harden their flesh like a tree with oak body. Masters of this mantle obtained true metabolism, enhancing their bodies to such a point that they became incredibly difficult to kill.

Appendix
References

Connections

---

## Phobia

A phobia was an overwhelming fear or anxiety towards something, a response that was typically arbitrary and uncontrollable.

Acquisition
Many phobias could be acquired when a stressful or terrifying encounter left lasting mental marks, causing the fear response to become its own monster.

Magical effects could simulate phobias, creating feelings of terror and avoidance that compelled certain courses of action. These included spells like antipathy and fear, and magical items like the rod of terror and wand of fear. Psionics users could achieve the same with the power phobia amplification.

Effects
What separated phobias from typical fears was their intensity and irrationality. The victim might well not believe in the validity of the fear, but would be affected regardless. When exposed to the source of the fear, they would feel a very strong aversion, anxiety, or revulsion towards the subject, and could flee or cower rather than approach, touch, or otherwise deal with it. They might even be averse to speaking of the concept.

Some common phobias included:
 Agoraphobia, the fear of open spaces.
 Astraphobia, the fear of storms.
 Claustrophobia, the fear of confined spaces or being trapped.
 Entomophobia, the fear of insects.
 Hemophobia, the fear of blood.
 Hypnophobia the fear of sleep.
 Monophobia, the fear of being alone.
 Necrophobia, the fear of corpses and dead things.
 Nyctophobia, the fear of the night or darkness.
 Onomatophobia, the fear of a specific name.
 Pnigerophobia, the fear of being smothered.
 Psychrophobia, the fear of cold.
 Xenophobia, the fear of strangers or foreigners.

Less mundane (but perhaps more rational) phobias included:
 Aberraphobia, the fear of aberrations.
 Dracophobia, the fear of dragons.
 Faephobia, the fear of the fey.
 Manaphobia, the fear of magic.
 Myxophobia, the fear of oozes.
 Phantasmaphobia, the fear of spectral undead.
 Teratophobia, the fear of monsters.

Notable Affected
 Most beholders had an unreasonably deep well of xenophobia and paranoia between their two minds that fueled their malevolence.
 Cherish Hollow, who was terrified of most things except for zombies.
 Jhesrhi Coldcreek, who developed a phobia of being touched by people after terrible mistreatment by ogres.
 Kheros, a warrior who developed a crippling fear of blood.
 Krimmon Amethystall, who had a phobia of water while the head of Tethyr's navy.
 Ogolai Thuunlakalaga had a deep fear of griffons from childhood trauma.
 Shadowheart, who developed a terrible fear of wolves and dogs, caused by partial memories emerging from her magically induced amnesia.
 Storn the prospector had a paranoid fear of white dragons—though not an entirely unreasonable one to have in Icewind Dale.
 Tymoriel, an adventurer who had a well-earned fear of lycanthropes.
 Wulfgang Warts, a war leader with a crippling fear of Phantom Pass and similar places.

## Phlog-crawler

A phlog-crawler was an eldritch lifeform living in wildspace.

Description
Phlog-crawlers resembled elongated clouds with limbs, most often eight, which made them look somewhat like spiders. They shimmered in many colors as they moved.

Behavior
The creatures attacked living things by wrapping around them and absorbing their lifeforce. They avoided open flames, except when their hunger was indeed great.

Abilities
Being amorphous and nearly weightless, they could slip through any crevice.

Combat
A phlog-crawler absorbed a target's lifeforce in a manner that reduced their physical strength; most people would last maybe ten minutes in contact with one of them, or more if they were stronger or more resistant to death magic. The creatures were barely affected by physical weapons, but fire instantly slew them on contact, having similar results to a rather basic fireball.

Ecology
Living both in wildspace and the phlogiston, the phlog-crawlers preferred the former for its abundance of prey. According to Torilian sages, these creatures may have been immortal. They could endure up to a day-length within an atmosphere, and the atmospheric envelopes of any object under one naval ton that were in contact with it turned into hazards in half the time.

Rumors
It was said that in distant spheres, a captive phlog-crawler was likely a suitable ingredient for a potion of longevity.

Appearances

## Phathroi

Phathroi was a dutiful baelnorn (or guardnorn) in the ruins of Myth Drannor up to the late 14 century DR.

History

Phathroi defended Myth Drannor's ruins for centuries until the Elven Crusade arrived to restore the city. Unfortunately, the Red Wizards of Thay also arrived to raid and rob, even finding an old Cult of the Dragon base hidden in the ruins where the Cult had once attempted a dracolich ritual. The dracolich Karliath had been left trapped and incomplete there only to be found by the unworshipful Red Wizards, who saw this as an opportunity to enslave a powerful undead minion. Realizing this was beyond even her abilities, Phathroi called on the Elves of Cormanthor at their nearby camp and directed them to strike at the Red Wizards before they could escape with Karliath in tow.

While Phathroi used her magic to seal the area and prevent their escape, the Elves and their allies broke into the horror-filled lair to finish the job. They fought their way through the remnants of the Eternal Brigade, the marilith Ophael, Grand Ritualist Kathul Sep, and finally met and destroyed Karliath himself, saving the world from the menace of yet another dracolich.

## Phaernos Bauldyn

Phaernos Bauldyn was the innkeeper of the Ambletrees Arms inn during the 3 century DR.

Relationships
He was friends with Braer.

History
One evening in Flamerule of the, Phaernos Bauldyn was standing at the front door of his inn when the priestess Elmara walked into his establishment, requesting shelter and warmth. She offered to pay with spells. Later, during the night, he woke her up, and she introduced herself. He revealed that his friend Braer had told him to shelter Elmara, should she ever come by.

## Phy'xzar

Phy'xzar was a drider serving House Argith that joined their expedition to Tethyamar in the 1480s DR.

History
During Talice Argith's attempts to obtain the jewels of ardor in Tethyamar, Phy'xzar was assigned the duty of keeping captured dwarves from the Tethyamar Mining Outpost, alongside Phyrsinil and Uhlsirayn. He put the dwarves to work mining more jewels of ardor, keeping two mind-controlled dwarves and two ogre battle thralls on him as a bodyguard. Eluf the Steadfast sent Rielle Proudforge and a team of surfacers to rescue the dwarves, and so the drider jailer was slain and his victims freed.

## Phargred Avithoul

Phargred Avithoul was a drider and slavemaster of House Avithoul from Sschindylryn during the 1480s DR.

History

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Phargred oversaw the operations at the ruined Temple of Mystra in the King's Forest as they sought after the Thread of the Weave, joined by Eldrin Szind and Odezzt Avithoul. Phargred used drow slave collars to control large numbers of slaves captured from Eveningstar, using them as labor and soldiers for the drow as they searched the ruins. He was greatly displeased to find a team of adventurers that had broken into the temple and rescued Ana Brabener along with the other slaves, killing Eldrin and Odezzt in the process. He attempted to bar their way out, accompanied by a large group of slaves, but was overcome and perished in battle.

## Phandalue Tarinthil

Phandalue Tarinthil was a human counter clerk for the Aurora's Realms Shop, High Road Catalogue Counter in the North Ward of Waterdeep in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
She was known as a stunning beauty.

Personality
Phandalue had a sharp tongue and no patience or time for those she deemed fools. Nevertheless, it seemed half the young noblemen of the city courted her.

## Phantom stalker

Phantom stalkers were beings native to the Elemental Plane of Fire. They typically appeared on the Prime Material Plane only when summoned by high-level magic-users, serving as guardians.

Description
In their raw elemental state, phantom stalkers manifested as slender columns of flame, with faint outlines suggesting limbs and a head—evoking the image of a gaunt fire elemental. When they appeared on the Prime Material Plane, their most frequent guise was that of a tall, muscular humanoid, over  in height, with a reddish hue and blazing eyes. Their faces resembled stretched human features, defined by a pointed chin and prominent forehead. If provoked, they shed their tangible form, taking on the appearance of a spectral blaze.

Personality
Once summoned, phantom stalkers protected their masters with unwavering loyalty, engaging in combat as bodyguards. However, wizards had to be exceedingly careful when giving instructions—phantom stalkers obeyed commands to the letter and often twisted vague orders, eager to find a loophole that allowed them to return to their fiery home.

This tendency was a troublesome trait inherited from their interactions with the efreet. Despite this quirk, phantom stalkers generally performed admirably as bodyguards.

Abilities
Phantom stalkers possessed the ability to alter their form and were known to manifest in a variety of shapes. Phantom stalkers possessed the ability to polymorph and frequently assumed various shapes, though most of their chosen forms retained the fiery palette of reds, yellows, and oranges. Regardless of form, they retained their capacity for flight—an ability unaffected by their altered physical shape.

These entities were capable of flight, and their aerial mobility remained intact regardless of the form they took, though their maneuverability did vary depending on the chosen shape.

They were completely immune to fire-based harm, and magical fire actually restored their vitality over time. Cold-based effects, by contrast, were far more dangerous, inflicting significant damage.

The phantom stalker had the ability to lower its body temperature to approximately, allowing it to handle or transport objects without setting them ablaze. It also possessed a formidable strength.

Combat
Phantom stalkers typically engaged foes using their razor-sharp claws, each capable of inflicting light wounds.

In moments of imminent defeat during battle, a phantom stalker could unleash its remaining life force in a final burst as a fireball, after which the stalker dissolved and perished. In desperate circumstances, some chose to detonate this fireball upon themselves, aiming to damage as many opponents as possible. However, this act of self-sacrifice was reserved strictly for last stands and was never carried out if it risked harming the summoner, unless that summoner bore direct responsibility for the phantom stalker's doom, such as sending it knowingly into a suicidal confrontation.

Society
On the Elemental Plane of Fire, phantom stalkers had no formal hierarchy or organization. In fact, they were frequently pushed around and dominated by more powerful entities such as fire elementals, efreet, and salamanders.

Phantom stalkers were conjured through a specialized variant of the invisible stalker spell. The incantation required a piece of coal intricately carved to resemble a flame; beyond that detail, the spell operated identically.

When summoned by a mage, a phantom stalker typically expressed gratitude for having escaped the hostile environment of its native plane and demonstrated a willingness to serve loyally. Yet, despite this relief, it also harbored a deep-seated resentment toward being forcibly conjured. This was an internal conflict shared by many dwellers of the Elemental Planes.

There was a singular directive embedded in the summoning of phantom stalkers: if their conjurer was slain, the stalkers immediately vanished. Then, after a delay of 1 to 4 hours, they returned with deadly intent, having precisely traced the killer's location via the Ethereal Plane. This tracking ability manifested only upon the death of their master and faded as soon as they located the individual responsible. Once the phantom stalker had located its summoner's killers, it returned to the Prime Material Plane, driven by a burning desire for retribution.

Usage
Phantom stalkers were also valued for the volatile liquid fire coursing through their bodies. When successfully harvested, this elemental essence was used in the forging of flame tongue swords, legendary weapons imbued with fiery power.

## Phoebe

Phoebe was a human commoner inhabitant of Neverwinter's Beggar's Nest in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Phoebe was a human woman with a pale complexion, brown eyes, and blonde hair. She appeared distraught and haggard after losing her home to the plague.

History
During the time the Wailing Death ravaged Neverwinter in the, Phoebe's home district, Beggar's Nest, was hit the hardest. Those who died of the plague were burned on the city streets, and, to make things worse, the district erupted in a zombie infestation. Phoebe managed to evacuate from the district before the Neverwintran militia quarantined it off. Now homeless, Phoebe had to sleep on city streets by a beggars' campfire in the City Core, where she mourned her old home and the fact that she couldn't wait for the plague to take her in the comfort of her own bed.

## Phantom panther

Phantom panthers were unearthly and dangerous feline creatures that resembled ghostly panthers. They were fearsome opponents but could be bound via magic into servitude.

Description
A phantom partner was a large panther with an eerie, ghostly appearance, almost incorporeal, its eyes glowing with an orange radiance. Despite their delicate appearance, they could move as fast and be as corporeal as any living panther.

Abilities
When a phantom partner moved, it left a phantasmic wake—a path glowing with ghostly flames that sapped enemies' physical strength, speed and dealt damage. When an adventurer managed to bind a phantom panther to them through magical means, the creature also boosted its master's awareness and accuracy.

History
Phantom panthers were known to have been used by some of the well-off adventurers in service of Neverwinter in the late 15 century DR.

## Phobia amplification

Phobia amplification was a psionic devotion of the telepathy discipline that allowed a psionicist to amplify one of a target's greatest fears into an irrational phobia.

Effects
Able to affect people from any distance, phobia amplification caused a target to believe that they were imminently in danger of their greatest fear, even if they were in a situation where it was impossible for that fear to be present. For example, a target with a fear of falling would be made to feel they were imminently in danger of falling over a cliff, even if they were standing in the middle of a prairie.

Reactions to this effect varied amongst individuals, but their actions would uniformly be centered around ensuring their protection from whatever object or situation they were fearful of. Some might still defend themselves when attacked (unless defending was somehow tied in to the phobia), others might freeze in place or flee. In the case of magic users like wizards, some would resort to using their spells to guarantee their safety or try to teleport away from the danger.

The effect of phobia amplification lasted for as long as the psionicist maintained it. Upon being ceased, the target's fear would fade back to whatever its base degree of fearfulness was.

Prerequisite Powers
In order to learn and make use of phobia amplification, a manifester first had to know and use the powers contact and mindlink in order to access the fears buried within a target's mind.

Users
This power was exhibited as an innate psionic ability amongst dhours and yochlol.

In psionics terms, phobia amplification was innately known by yuan-ti.

Notable Users
 Ryvvik Dlardrageth, a cambion psionicist.
 The Watchers, a group of illithids that served as the early-warning system for the Underdark community of Nar'Vheen, included phobia amplification among their repertoire of powers to sus out the greatest fears of intruders.

## Pharaoh's mace

The pharaoh's mace was an enchanted mace of unknown origins found on the Sword Coast North in the late 14 century DR.

Powers
The pharaoh's mace possessed a +2 enchantment that made the weapon deadlier and guided the wielder's hand toward the target. It allowed users adept at turning undead, like clerics, to use these powers more often. Its hits could also slay some undead instantaneously.

History
The exact origins of the mace were unknown, but its name suggests a relationship with the pharaoh rulers of the empire of Mulhorand or the leaders of Bakar.

In the Year of Lightning Storms, 1374 DR, one such weapon was found in the town of Port Llast, where it was sold by Haljal to the friends of the Church of Waukeen.
